The reviewer's questions, decided by Mike, 2026-11-14: the fallback jobs limit is a fixed 64 MiB, which can exceed free − reserve on a small machine; it is capped at free − reserve when free is known, in step 6 (what a zero result means for jobs is settled there). When the jobs group cannot be made, jobs go under init's own group at 16 MiB each: accepted, and item 7 now says so. `bin/hello`'s probe modes ship in the production image: accepted until dev-boot has its own image. Whether the SDK run checks `memory-test slots`' exit status is checked in step 6.

The reviewer's answers on teardown cost: a teardown takes one step per 2 MiB of mapped range whether or not its pages were touched, but making that range costs the job as many map steps of its own; others pay for the teardown one step per entry; the Process, TaskGroup, region and object slots stay held meanwhile.

Checked (development tier): kernel host tests, `xtask clippy`, `kernel-check` on Deepwyrm `556601a`; host tests of system-init, launch-proto and xtask, `xtask test fmt` and `clippy` on Wyrmroot `2f2329f`; `dev-boot --check` on one CPU (longest step 7.3 ms, a commit) and `--smp` (35.9 ms, a fill-in) on that pair, run with Wyrmroot's fixes uncommitted. `--sdk` was not run: the SDK did not change. The two kernel fixes have no host test, as the live facade has no host harness (§10.10 item 11).

### 10.12 Step 6: stepped finalization of an on-demand backing: design (2026-11-14)

From the coordinator's read of Deepwyrm `4b4dd5c`; paths are under `kernel/src/`. It answers step-5 review K R-1.

**Today.** The cleanup drain (`drain_cleanup_queue`, T and P held, interrupts off) finalizes a MemoryObject in `PayloadFinalizer::finalize_one` → `MemoryObjectAuthority::take_finalization` → `complete_memory_finalization` → `FrameRoleManager::cancel_object_backing` → `release_index`. That first walks the whole index to check it (`index_count`, 512 reads per node), then frees every node and committed page in one recursion. Its frames are bounded by the object's charge (pages and nodes, §8.4), so by its TaskGroup's limit: 512 MiB is about 131,000 frames in one hold. The registry slot stays `Finalizing` until `complete_payload_finalization`, and `charged_pages` counts a finalizing slot's weight, so the group's usage falls only at the end.

**Design.**
1. **Owed, not done.** In `finalize_one`, an on-demand object whose charged weight is above `FINALIZE_INLINE_FRAMES` (64) is not completed: its `MemoryObjectFinalization` (final release, backing grant, kind) is kept in an owed-finalization queue in `MemoryObjectAuthority`, a FIFO of at most `OBJECTS` entries, and the chain goes on. Its authority slot stays occupied (record and backing kept, marked *releasing*), so the queue is bounded by the slots and a new object cannot take the slot while its frames are still held; every lookup that resolves a record by object id treats a releasing record as absent (the registry slot is already `Finalizing`, so no handle or reference reaches it, and this is defence in depth). Objects at or below the line are finalized in place as today: at most 64 frames and their nodes' reads.
2. **One step.** A new `FrameRoleManager::release_index_step(head, cursor, budget)` frees the index in object-page order from `cursor`: each committed page found is unlinked and freed; an absent entry skips its whole span; when the cursor passes the end of a node's span, that node, now empty, is freed and its parent entry cleared (never the root). It visits at most `FINALIZE_STEP_ENTRIES` (512, matching the 2 MiB teardown step) entries, counting a freed node as one, and returns the new cursor, the frames freed and whether the index below the root is empty. Nothing can commit into a finalizing object (no handle, lease, DMA mapping or lookup remains), so entries behind the cursor stay empty and no full check is needed per step; each entry is still checked against its root and place by the walk, as today.
3. **The charge falls as it goes.** Each step lowers the finalizing slot's weight by the frames it freed, through a new registry helper (checked subtraction, `Finalizing` slots only, the counterpart of §10.9's helper for live ones), so the group's usage falls step by step, as a stepped teardown's does (§10.10), and init's settle (Wyrmroot `0f723d2`) sees it.
4. **Completion.** When the index below the root is empty, the root is freed and `complete_memory_finalization`'s remaining work runs: `complete_payload_finalization`, the authority slot freed, `LIVE_ON_DEMAND_OBJECTS` lowered. The slot's weight is then the root's one frame, which completion clears with the tag.
5. **Where it runs.** One step per `drain_finalizers`, after `release_unmapped_step` and before the owed teardown step, not gated by the teardown allowance: like release-on-unmap's step it needs no particular address space, only T and P and the direct map. Every syscall return, refused fault and BSP timer safe point drains, so a dead job's memory is freed by others' entries one step at a time. With nothing else to run, the idle BSP also steps it: `idle_work_remains` counts owed finalizations, and the idle loop takes the step on the kernel root without the publisher-root switch that teardown needs, keeping the interrupt window (§10.10 item 5) between steps.
6. **Exhaustion.** The fault arm that frees owed memory first (§10.10 item 7, `Exhausted` while owed → a drain → `Released`) also fires while finalization is owed; syscalls still refuse (Mike, 2026-11-14, "faults retry, syscalls refuse").
7. **Timed.** `StepCall::Finalize` in the step report, so dev-boot's `[steps]` lines show the longest finalization step.
8. **Tests (host).** A large on-demand object finalized over several drains: the frames, the weight and the group's usage fall by each step's frames and end at zero, with `check_indexes` whole after every step; a sparse index (one page per leaf node) frees its nodes as the cursor passes them; an object at the inline line finalizes in one drain; a releasing slot is absent to lookups and is not reused before completion; an owed object whose group is gone still completes.

**Design review (2026-11-14).** Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5 at low effort, against `a4cd8b4`; checked against the code by the coordinator.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | Medium | The primordial terminal teardown (`finish_terminal_teardown`) drains and then proves every registry slot free (`prove_registry_capacity`); an object left owed there fails it | Fixed: that path completes every owed finalization before the proof |
| D2 | Medium | A node's span can end past the object, so a cursor that stops at the object's end never "passes" the last node at each level: nodes leak or the object never completes | Fixed: a node ends at its span's end clamped to the object, so reaching the end frees the whole last path |
| D3 | Low | A step whose budget runs out at a node's end must free that node in the same step, or a later step frees its parent with a child linked | Fixed: node frees cascade upward before the budget is checked; a step may visit up to three entries past its budget |
| D4 | Low | The finalize step runs inside timed steps (family-G drains), and one timing slot per CPU would end the outer step early; `CALL_BITS` holds 8 calls | Fixed: a step already timed on its CPU counts it; `CALL_BITS` is 4 |
| D5 | Low | Item 1 kept the backing grant in two places | Fixed: the queued finalization holds record and grant; the slot is held by the owed entry, which slot allocation checks |
| R1 | Medium | A job that drops a large object and at once makes an eager create or commit in the same budget is refused until enough steps run (faults retry) | Accepted by Mike, 2026-11-14: syscalls refuse |
| R2 | Low/Medium | The inline line (64 frames) did not bound a drain: many sparse objects of 64 frames each read about 24K entries | Fixed: no inline line; every on-demand finalization is owed and the step's budget bounds a drain |
| R3 | Low | One object per drain would hold many slots | Fixed: a step's unspent budget goes on to the next owed object |
| R4 | Low | The fault arm retries while any finalization is owed, though another job's: an over-budget job spins instead of ending | Accepted (Low): bounded by the queue, which every drain shortens; it spends the faulting job's own time |
| R5 | Low | With the BSP running user code that makes no syscalls, steps come one per BSP tick, and init's 500 ms settle can end first | Measured below (`owed-longest-us`) |
| R6 | Low | Without the whole-tree check before freeing, a corrupt index panics partway through instead of before | Accepted (Low): a panic either way |

**As built (revision 2).**
1. *Owed.* `finalize_one` takes the finalization and hands it to `MemoryObjectAuthority::defer_finalization`, which queues every on-demand one (FIFO, `OBJECTS` entries, one per slot) and returns any other kind for completion at once. The record is out of the authority's tables, so every lookup misses it; the owed entry holds a slot, which `validate_backing`'s slot search skips.
2. *Step.* `FrameRoleManager::release_index_step` (D2, D3). `finalize_owed_step` spends `FINALIZE_STEP_ENTRIES` (512) over the queue oldest first, lowering each object's weight by its freed frames through `ObjectRegistry::lower_finalizing_charge_pages` and completing each whose cursor reached its end (`complete_memory_finalization`, which frees the empty root).
3. *Where.* Last in every `drain_finalizers`, after the teardown step and its drain, so a teardown's finalizations share the drain's step. The idle BSP takes one on the kernel root (`idle_finalize_step`), and `idle_work_remains` counts owed finalizations. The fault arm fires for owed finalization too.
4. *Report.* `[steps]` gains `release` and `finalize` calls; per call its longest step, its steps and their total (`call-longest-us`, `call-steps`, `call-total-us`); and `owed-longest-us`, the longest stretch teardown, and finalization, stayed owed without the queue emptying, sampled at each drain.

**Measurements (the rest of step 6).** The step report gains a `Release` call for §10.9's release step (its lease-pool scans, item 8) and a `Finalize` call (item 7). Teardown latency (§10.10 item 8) is the time from a job's termination to its group's usage settling, read by dev-boot from `memory` samples. Probe 14's per-page cost is broken down by step call. Init's worst reply delay after a settle pause (step-5 review W R1) is measured with 16 sessions.

**Measured (2026-11-14, development tier, QEMU TCG, not hardware times).** From the `[steps]` report of `dev-boot --check` on Deepwyrm `0ac2147`, Wyrmroot `e3d06a1`, SDK `56eb579`; the measurement changed from the design above in one way: teardown latency is the kernel's own `owed-longest-us` (the longest stretch the queue stayed non-empty, sampled at each drain), not `memory` samples, which are 0.5 s apart.

| | One CPU | `--smp` (4) | `--sdk` |
|---|---|---|---|
| Longest finalize step | 0.46 ms | 0.88 ms | 0.51 ms |
| Longest release step (§10.9 item 8) | 0.52 ms | 0.86 ms | 1.8 ms |
| Longest teardown step | 5.4 ms | 38.9 ms | 7.7 ms |
| Finalization owed, longest stretch | 0.18 s | 0.90 s | 0.14 s |
| Teardown owed, longest stretch | 0.23 s | 2.47 s | 1.68 s |

- *K R-1 closed.* Before, a 512 MiB object's finalization freed about 131,000 frames in one hold; now no finalize step exceeded 0.9 ms. *(Correction, 2026-11-15, §10.19: that figure covered only the steps the report counted. Over all steps the longest is 0.79 ms on one CPU and up to 2.52 ms with `--smp`, the wait for P included.)*
- *§10.9 item 8 closed.* The release step's lease-pool scans stayed under 2 ms; building the intervals once per step is not needed.
- *§10.10 item 8 measured.* With the probes ending jobs back to back and the hogs running, the teardown queue stayed non-empty up to 2.5 s on four CPUs: a bound on one dead job's wait, not its typical wait. Steps come from others' entries, so the time depends on load, as §10.10 says. The longest teardown step on four CPUs, 38.9 ms, includes waits for P and T inside the timed step; the one-CPU figure (5.4 ms) is the step's own work.
- *Probe 14's per-page cost, closed.* Per call, total over steps (one CPU), each step at most 2 MiB: map 1.2 ms a step (about 2.4 µs a page), unmap 1.6 ms (3.1 µs), protect 1.9 ms (3.8 µs), teardown 1.3 ms (2.5 µs). A 256 MiB eager create takes about 40 ms (zeroing through the direct map) and a 4 MiB map 7 ms, so L6's "44 ms fixed cost" is gone: map, protect and unmap cost page-table work per page. Of the 0.39 s a 256 MiB map takes beside the CPU hogs, its steps hold about 0.16 s; the rest is time given to the hogs between steps (§10.8).
- *Init's reply after a settle pause (step-5 review W R1).* Measured by a host test with 16 sessions waiting through a whole pause: the last answered 875 ms after it began (one session per 25 ms tick), against wyrmsh's 1 s. *Mike (2026-11-14): drain after a pause.* In the tick a pause ends every session takes a turn (Wyrmroot `420b2fb`); the test now has all 16 answered in that tick, about 500 ms after the pause began.

### 10.13 Step 6: second kernel security pass and as built (2026-11-14)

Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5, which reported **low** effort though asked for the second pass at default effort; at Deepwyrm `0ac2147` (`0ac214736b88b54b623d69353bd70425f47cd22a`), covering the four areas §10.11 left (§9) and §10.12's code. Findings checked against the code by the coordinator.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | High | A blocking wait leaves its wait control `Pending` until `prepare_suspend`; a remote Stop taken at `settle_dispatched_control` before that reaches the precommit with the continuation suspended, and `retire_idle_for_stop` accepted only `Idle` and `Resuming`: the kernel panicked ("remote stop suspended idle control drifted before precommit"). Seen once in an `--smp` dev-boot (`terminate` of a job mid create-loop); four more passed. Predates M5; every drain's extra work since `0ac2147` widened the window | Fixed: Deepwyrm `56d9081`. The precommit retires a pending suspension too and stages the quantum cancellation `prepare_suspend` would have; host test `a_remote_stop_retires_a_pending_suspension` (fails before the fix) |
| D-2 | Low | A finalize completion re-reads the root's 512 entries (`release_index`) but was charged one: one step could complete about 256 small objects, about 131,000 reads | Fixed: `56d9081`, a completion is charged 512, so a step completes about one object |
| D-3 | Low | `kernel-check` built its call graph from relocations, and a self-call in a function's own section leaves none: `index_free_node`, `index_count_node`, `observe_empty_owned_subtree` and `reclaim_disconnected_subtree` recursed unseen | Fixed: `6215514`. It also finds rel32 calls and jumps to a function's own start, and lists the four, each bounded by four levels. The thread stack bound is unchanged (246,984 of 393,216 bytes) |
| D-4 | Low | Two doc comments sat on the wrong functions after an insertion | Fixed: `56d9081` |
| Q1 | — | Other Stop checkpoints that run with the control `Pending` or `Clear` while the CPU's suspended claim is set | None found beyond `settle_dispatched_control`; the `TerminateCurrent` resume goes to the terminal reaper |
| Q2 | — | The terminal teardown completes owed finalizations, not owed teardowns | Already recorded: §10.10 item 6 (not built, R-4), never reached in a WYR1 product |

The pass's answers on its four areas: (1) stack depth: the new code is iterative with fixed arrays; D-3 was the gap. (2) APs never take teardown or finalize steps (BSP only), and the BSP's polling loops service the rendezvous latch every pass; D-1 is on the syscall path, not in idle. (3) No child AddressRegion can exist at this revision: every region owns its address space and the ABI has no sub-region call; mapping into an exited Process is refused, so nothing re-maps between teardown steps. If sub-regions come, teardown must change with them. (4) Holds of T and P in one drain are each bounded: cleanup pops, one release step (up to 4,096 entries, the largest by structure work), one teardown step (2 MiB), one finalize step (512 entries plus a completion), plus any family-G step the drain runs inside. Nothing found lets a job make one unbounded.

**Step 6 as built.** Deepwyrm `0ac2147` (stepped finalization, the step report), `56d9081` and `6215514` (the pass's fixes); Wyrmroot `e3d06a1` (the fallback jobs limit capped at free − reserve, Mike: a zero limit launches no job, refused as `Capacity`), `4f4d2b6` and `420b2fb` (the reply delay, measured and fixed); the SDK unchanged at `56eb579`. `memory-test slots`' exit status was already checked: `run_sdk` requires `application=0`.

**Checked (development tier).** Kernel host tests (1,341), `xtask clippy`, `format` and `kernel-check` on Deepwyrm `6215514`; Wyrmroot's system-init (139) and xtask tests, `xtask test fmt` and `clippy` on `420b2fb`. `dev-boot --check` on that pair: one CPU passed (longest step 5.3 ms, teardown), `--sdk` passed (26.1 ms, a commit), and `--smp` passed twice and failed once (below).

**Stopped under the stop rule (2026-11-14).** The third `--smp` boot panicked on CPU 3 at shutdown, after the last command: "remote stop released execution ownership before deferred cleanup quiesced" (`arch/x86_64/rendezvous.rs:642`, `commit_prepared_stop_at_safe_point`). The assertion is `deferred_cleanup_is_quiescent` (`primordial.rs`): the cleanup queue empty, rendezvous cleanup staged, the stopped Thread's service state quiescent (no operation owner, wait control clear), no deferred current, and no Running claim of the stopped Thread on that CPU. Which of these failed is not known: the panic does not say. No earlier retained log shows it. It is the third failure of one class, a remote Stop meeting a Thread in the middle of handing itself into or out of a suspension:
1. `f3a/d1h3-normal-01/smp`: a Stop at the precommit while the idle poll's resume was short of its handoff (`Resuming`), fixed then by keeping that state for the precommit to retire.
2. This step's D-1: a Stop at `settle_dispatched_control` while the wait control was still `Pending`, fixed by `56d9081` the same way.
3. This assertion, in the commit after such a precommit, on the first boots with `56d9081`. Whether it follows from (2)'s fix (a pending suspension now reaching a commit whose cleanup expected `prepare_suspend` to have run) is not known.

What they share: the precommit and commit decide from the CPU's suspended claim and its wait control, which `accept`, `prepare_suspend`, the idle poll and the resume each move separately, and each fix so far taught the stop one more intermediate state. No fourth fix is made. The evidence is kept: `.tmp/dev-boot/1794694412071081946` (D-1's panic) and `.tmp/dev-boot/1794695507441309393` (this one), COM1 and COM2 logs. Next, for Mike: a diagnostic build that names the failed condition, repeated `--smp` boots to reproduce it, and an independent design review of the stop and suspension handoff as a whole (every state the control and the claims pass through, and what the stop must do in each) before any fix. M5 does not close while this stands.

**Diagnosis (2026-11-14, Mike: "proceed with the diagnostic build and design review").** Lane `lane/stop-diag` (Deepwyrm `60d965b`, `7fbf0b3`): the quiescence assertion names its failed condition, and while teardown stays owed past 3 s the kernel reports why its steps were skipped. 22 `--smp` boots on it, Wyrmroot `420b2fb`.
- *The shutdown panic did not recur* (0 of 22; 1 of about 30 boots in all). The independent design review (Claude Opus 5.5, low effort, at `7b183ab`) found 56d9081's retire of a `Pending` control consistent with every condition of the commit's check, and its best explanation (about 55%) is outside the wait states: the commit requires the *shared* cleanup queue empty, the stopped CPU releases T for its guard-free CR3 switch between precommit and commit, and holds that return anything but `ReturnToCaller` (`ReturnReady::without_return`) end without draining, though the comment at `primordial.rs:4606` ("design §11.2.12 rule 4") requires every hold to. Both code facts checked by the coordinator. The review also found D-B (Medium): a Stop on a resumed Thread whose operation is not yet settled relies on the initiator having cleaned it; and D-C (Low): Clear with a suspended claim has no precommit case (unreachable today). It proposes one classification of the handoff state with the stop's actions derived from it, four invariants and a host test per state.
- *A second failure, three times in 22 boots: a dead job's teardown owed for over 3 s, up to 6 s,* and once the shell's `terminate` missing wyrmsh's 1 s deadline (`wait` then answered `foreign`). In every report the only skips were "no allowance" (1) and "queue empty" (2); residents were zero. So teardown is not blocked: it is slow. `churn-map` keeps 64 mappings of one 256 MiB object, 16 GiB of mapped range for 256 MiB of budget, and teardown visits mapped range 2 MiB a step (about 8,192 steps), one step per BSP timer allowance, each paid by whoever runs on the BSP, often init. The earlier step-6 run whose usage stayed at 256 MiB for 10 s is the same. This is the third failure of one class (a dead job's teardown cost landing on init: step 2's stall, step 4's deadline, this), so it too is stopped before a fix.

### 10.14 Tearing a dead root down by tables: design (2026-11-14)

*Mike (2026-11-14): drop whole tables.* From the coordinator's read of Deepwyrm `b3d4863`; paths under `kernel/src/`. To be reviewed independently before it is built.

**Today.** `owed_teardown_step` → `unmap_inactive_pieces` runs `AddressRegion::unmap` over the first mapping, at most 2 MiB a step, through a page-table publisher that clears each leaf; the region's transaction releases the removed leases, whose held pins `settle_locally` releases at once (no CPU is resident). When no mapping remains, `retire_owed_root` → `teardown_child_root_under_initiator` → `teardown_empty_child_address_space` proves the whole hierarchy empty (`observe_empty_owned_subtree`, every entry of every table) and reclaims it (`reclaim_disconnected_subtree`) in one hold. Cost: one step per 2 MiB of *mapped range*, touched or not; 16 GiB of range is about 8,192 steps, and the final proof reads every entry of every table in one hold.

**Why leaves need not be cleared.** A dead root that is owed teardown is exited, quiescent and has no resident CPU (`owed_teardown_step` checks all three). PCID and global pages are off (`mm/activation.rs`, DW0-C2), so a CR3 load flushes every user translation and paging-structure entry: no CPU can walk this root or hold anything cached from it, which is the argument today's `settle_locally` already rests on. A leaf maps a frame owned by a MemoryObject, never by the table; the object's frames are freed only through its leases' and handles' releases (finalization §10.12, release on unmap §10.9). So once the region's records and leases are released, the table frames can be freed with their leaf entries unread: a stale leaf in a table no CPU can walk is inert, whatever later happens to the frame it names.

**Design.**
1. **Leases first, by mapping.** A new quiesced-teardown operation on the region removes one whole mapping per step (not 2 MiB of it) through the same transaction as `unmap` — the same lease replacement, §10.9 removed-translation hulls and coverage pins, and `MappingFinalReleases` settled locally — with a *detached* publisher that writes no leaf, allocates no table and reclaims none. Its cost is the transaction's bookkeeping, independent of the mapping's size. A region holds at most its mapping slots (64 for jobs), so this phase is at most that many steps.
2. **Then tables, by count.** With no mapping left, a stepped reclaim frees the root's user-half tables bottom-up, at most `TEARDOWN_STEP_TABLES` (64) per step, from a cursor over the PML4's user entries: a PT is freed without reading its entries; a PD or PDPT is read (512 entries) to find its children, freed when its last child is, and its parent entry cleared, as `release_index_step` does for the on-demand index (§10.12). The frame-role checks each table frame's role, owner and level as `reclaim_disconnected_subtree` does. Tables' charges, if any, fall as they are freed (to confirm in review: how a job's tables are charged).
3. **Then the root.** With the user half empty, `teardown_empty_child_address_space`'s proof reads only the PML4's user entries, all zero, and frees it as today.
4. **Where.** Unchanged: the owed-teardown step, its allowance and its places (§10.10), its 2 MiB `FAMILY_G_STEP_BYTES` replaced by "one mapping" in phase 1 and 64 tables in phase 2. The synchronous paths that stay synchronous (the bootstrap's, boot) are unchanged.
5. **Tests (host).** A root with large eager mappings (and aliases of one object) torn down: the number of steps is the mappings plus tables/64, not range/2 MiB; every lease released and pin settled; every table frame freed once and the role table whole after each step; the object's frames freed only when its last lease goes; a partially torn root resumed from its cursor; a PT whose leaves are still present freed without being read.

**Questions for review.** Whether any code reads or validates leaf entries of a table it frees (role checks, the journal, `check_invariants`); whether the detached transaction keeps every §10.9 invariant when the leaves stay in place until phase 2; whether a dead root can be the publisher root or be selected for return validation during phase 2; how table charges work.

**Review of §10.14 (2026-11-14).** Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5 at low effort, against `d92ead3`; checked by the coordinator. The core argument was confirmed: PCID and global pages are refused at activation and the publisher refuses a global leaf; residency is entered before a CR3 load and cleared only after the switch-away flush, the stop path's guard-free window included; a freed table frame is zeroed before any reuse; nothing reads a dead root's leaves after phase 1 except phase 3's proof; a whole-mapping transaction costs the same at any size (`rebuild`, `prepare_replace` and `commit` have no per-page work); the bootstrap root is unaffected.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| D-1 | Medium | No frame-role operation frees one table of a live hierarchy: `reclaim_preflighted_table` needs an `EmptyTableHierarchyGrant`, which only a proof of empty tables gives. Phase 2 needs a new per-table reclaim that lowers the root's `tables` count, refuses a table with child records (none are counted per table today) and checks owner, level and parent | Revision 2, item 2 |
| D-2 | Medium | The residency gate stays open until phase 3, so phases 1–2 rest on a residents read in another P hold; a wrong resident would walk freed, reused table frames | Revision 2, item 0 |
| D-3 | Medium | A publisher that writes no leaf breaks `AddressSpacePublisher`'s contract (publish exactly `after`); the synchronous bootstrap path shares `unmap_inactive_pieces(step: None)` | Revision 2, item 1 |
| D-4 | Low | A synchronous teardown of a root left by a detached phase 1 would fail its proof and panic; unreachable today | Revision 2, item 4: asserted |
| D-5 | Low | Table charges do not fall per table: fault- and fill-in-built tables are in the Process's weight, eager-map tables are uncharged, and the weight goes at Process finalization | Revision 2, item 2: phase 2 touches no charge |
| R-1 | Low | Counting tables does not bound reads: each PD or PDPT scanned costs 512 | Revision 2, item 2: entries read are counted; the cursor is rebuilt from the tables each step |
| R-2 | Low | §8.4's bound on uncharged eager-map tables (1/512 of backing) does not hold with aliases: 64 aliases make it 1/8, about 64 MiB of tables for a 512 MiB budget | Accepted (Low): bounded by mapping slots, and phase 2 frees them in about 256 steps where today takes about 8,192 |
| R-3 | Low | A large leaf in a PD must be refused, not read as a table | Revision 2, item 2, with a test |
| R-4 | Low | `reserve_teardown` is one global slot | Revision 2, item 2: each step takes and drops it in its own hold, with the `pins_hold_any` check |

**Revision 2 (supersedes items 1–4 above).**
0. **Retiring.** At its first step a queued root's binding moves one way into a retiring state in which `AddressSpaceCoherency::enter` is refused; every step re-reads residents in the same P hold as its work and requires zero.
1. **Leases first, by mapping, through a sealed teardown publisher.** A distinct type, not an `AddressSpacePublisher` that skips writes: constructible only from a quiescence proof, for an owned (never the primordial) binding in the retiring state. The region removes one whole mapping per step through the same transaction and settles its releases locally. `unmap_inactive_pieces(step: None)` (the synchronous bootstrap path) keeps the clearing publisher.
2. **Then tables.** A new unsafe frame-role operation frees one user table of a retiring hierarchy: it checks role, owner, level and parent, refuses a table that still has child records, lowers the root's `tables`, and is called bottom-up. Each step rebuilds its cursor from the tables (the first non-zero PML4 user entry, then the first non-zero PDPT and PD entry below it), frees PTs without reading them, reads PDs and PDPTs, refuses a large leaf, and stops after 512 entries read or 64 tables freed, whichever comes first. It takes and drops the pin reservation in its own hold. Charges are untouched: tables' usage falls at Process finalization, as today.
3. **Then the root,** whose proof now reads only the PML4's user entries.
4. **A queued root is torn down only by steps;** a synchronous teardown of a retiring root is refused by an assertion.
5. **Logic placement and tests.** Phase 2 lives in `AddressSpaceRootBindings` and `FrameRoleManager`, which have host fixtures. Tests: the per-table reclaim refuses live children and wrong owner, level, parent or the PML4, and brings `tables` to zero, after which the empty-hierarchy proof passes with none observed; a counting page-table target shows a PT freed without being read and each step's reads bounded; a freed PT with leaves, reused as a candidate, reads zero; the teardown publisher cannot be built for the primordial binding or with residents, and `enter` is refused once retiring; a synchronous teardown of a retiring root fails closed; the Process's weight is unchanged across phase 2; a large leaf in a PD is refused.

**Progress rule (amends revision 2, item 2; from the coordinator's read-only pass, 2026-11-14, recorded on Mike's go-ahead; not independently reviewed).** As item 2 is written a step can end without freeing anything. Rebuilding the cursor reads entries, those reads count against the step's 512, and reaching the first table can take up to 256 (PML4 user entries) + 512 (PDPT) + 512 (PD) = 1,280 of them. A root whose lowest remaining mapping sits high in its tables, a stack near the top of the user half for one, spends the budget on the descent; the next step rebuilds the same cursor and does the same, so the root is never retired and the queue never empties. The rule: **a step always frees at least one table.** The 512-entry bound ends a step only after its first free. The descent either reaches a PT, which is freed unread, or finds a PD or PDPT with no child, which is freed itself, so the first free comes within 1,280 reads and no step reads more than that before it, whatever the job mapped. No cursor is kept between steps. Host test: a root whose only mapping is at the last PML4 user entry, last PDPT entry and last PD entry is torn down in a bounded number of steps, each freeing at least one table and none reading more than the bound.

**As built (2026-11-15, M5 step 7).** Deepwyrm `c1537d7` (`c1537d71fa4476fca193b83fba29e306fc5a85b8`), written by a worker (Claude Opus 5.5) in lane `lane/m5s7-teardown` from `33cc63b`, reviewed by the coordinator and merged as `fdaa5a4` (main had moved by a plan-only commit; the kernel sources are `c1537d7`'s).

0. *Retiring.* `AddressSpaceCoherency` gains a one-way `retiring` flag: `begin_retiring` requires zero residents and an idle phase under the coherency lock, and after it `enter` and `prepare_mutation` are refused. The bindings refuse it for the primordial root. Each step's residents read, the move into retiring, the pin check and the work share one P hold (`retiring_teardown_step`).
1. *Mappings.* `AddressRegion::remove_mapping_detached` runs `unmap`'s transaction through a private publication seam whose detached side publishes nothing. `RetiringRootPublisher` (a sealed `DetachedTeardown`, not an `AddressSpacePublisher`) is built only from the Process's own quiescence proof for an owned, retiring binding with no resident, and borrows the bindings. The synchronous path keeps the clearing publisher.
2. *Tables.* `FrameRoleManager::check_retiring_table` and the unsafe `reclaim_retiring_table`; `AddressSpaceRootBindings::teardown_tables_step` (`activation/retiring_root.rs`) rebuilds its path from the PML4 each step, frees page tables unread, reads PDs and PDPTs, clears each freed table's parent entry and refuses a large leaf. It stops once it has freed a table and has freed 64 or read 512 entries, so the first free comes within 1,280 reads (the progress rule).
3. *The root.* For a retiring root the proof reads only the PML4's user entries.
4. *Steps only.* The synchronous teardown asserts its root is not retiring, and `root_for_process` refuses a retiring root.

*Deviations and choices.* A table's count of direct children reuses `RoleSlot.tables` (on a PML4 it still counts every table below), so `reclaim_preflighted_table` lowers the parent's count too when the parent is still live. A step that finds no mapping and no table retires the root, so a root takes mappings + table steps + 1. Phase 1 takes mappings in slot order. Within a step the walk continues from its place; nothing is kept between steps. A step refused for a resident now counts in `call-steps teardown`. Where steps run is unchanged (§10.10 item 2).

*Tests (host), ten.* The per-table reclaim's refusals and bottom-up frees; the retiring state's refusals; one transaction per whole mapping with aliases; a page table freed with its leaves reads zero when reused; page tables freed unread with each step bounded (102 tables in 4 steps); the progress rule's root (one mapping at the last entry of each level: 3 steps, 1,280 reads at most); a large leaf refused; who may get a teardown publisher; a synchronous teardown of a retiring root fails closed; the Process's weight unchanged. The carrier's wiring has no host harness: boots only.

**Checked (development tier).** By the coordinator at `c1537d7`: kernel host tests (1,364), `xtask clippy`, `kernel-check` (thread stack 232,952 of 393,216 bytes, reaper 173,040 of 196,608) and `fmt`. Boots by the worker, Wyrmroot `420b2fb`, QEMU TCG. Teardown owed, longest stretch:

| | `b9095c5` | `c1537d7` |
|---|---|---|
| One CPU | 0.23 to 0.26 s | 0.063 s |
| `--smp` | 1.2 to 5.2 s (6.9 s seen at `eb1bd36`) | 0.056 to 0.22 s |

No `wait … status=foreign` in 41 `--smp` boots of the change. Of the 21 at `c1537d7` itself, 18 passed, 2 failed dev-boot's check that a Stop landed inside a map, and 1 panicked.

- *The map check is the probe's gap, not the kernel's.* It counts a `churn-map` kill only as a Stop between two map steps or as an ended parked map call. A diagnostic build (lane `lane/m5s7-teardown-diag`, `69b450b`, not integrated; 20 boots) showed kills also land at a call's first safe point and between two calls, which nothing counts; that happens to the other churn jobs at the baseline too. 2 uncounted in 41 against 0 in 30 baseline boots is within chance, and the first six boots' pattern did not repeat. Which uncounted point the two failures hit was not caught. *Scheduled (§9): the probe kills `churn-map` more than once.*
- *The panic is R2, live.* `.tmp/dev-boot/1794720719145830826`, CPU 1, during a `terminate`: "remote stop precommit cannot classify its handoff (a prepared switch's outgoing Thread, its destination already running)". The first live sighting of §10.15's audit R2, older than this change and owned by §10.16.

### 10.15 No hold ends with the shared cleanup queue non-empty: design (2026-11-14)

*Mike (2026-11-14): fix by design.* Answers §10.13's shutdown panic (unconfirmed, review D-A) and the review's D-B and D-C. To be reviewed independently before it is built.

**The rule today.** A remote Stop's commit asserts the *shared* cleanup queue empty (`deferred_cleanup_is_quiescent`), and design §11.2.12 rule 4 (`primordial.rs:4606`) says every hold that releases T must leave it so. `ready_to_return` drains only for `ReturnToCaller` (`ReturnReady::without_return`); `TerminateCurrent`, `CompleteRemoteStop`, `ServiceRendezvous`, `SuspendCurrent` and `YieldCurrent` return without a drain, and other holds (faults, timer and idle paths, the reaper, the stop's own precommit and commit) end wherever they end. Only `TerminateCurrent` has a stated reason: its releases may finalize what the CPU still runs on, so the terminal handoff drains after its deferred reclaim.

**Design.**
1. **One way to end a hold.** Every release of the runtime authority T goes through one function that, before releasing, drains the shared cleanup queue (`drain_finalizers`) unless the hold is one that may not drain, and then asserts it empty. The runtime guard's release is made the only way out, so a new path cannot skip it.
2. **Holds that may not drain stage privately.** A hold that ends in `TerminateCurrent` (and any other the review finds unsafe to drain) moves whatever it pushed into a per-CPU outbox, which only that CPU's next drain empties into the shared queue, so the shared queue is still empty when T is released. The terminal handoff's first hold drains the outbox with the deferred reclaim, as today.
3. **The stop's commit checks what is its own.** `deferred_cleanup_is_quiescent` keeps its local conditions and keeps "shared queue empty", which items 1–2 now make true by construction; the staged rendezvous cleanup is unchanged.
4. **The handoff, classified once (review §5b).** In the precommit, one function reads the CPU's suspended and Running claims, `physically_executing`, the wait control and the stopped Thread's F-service operation owner, and returns the handoff state (running; pending without or with a replacement; idle; switch prepared; resuming; resumed with its operation unsettled) or panics naming the fields. The stop's actions (which claim to retire, where the quantum ticket comes from, whether to retire the control, and whether to transfer service cleanup — whenever an operation owner exists, in both branches, closing D-B) are a function of that state. Clear with a suspended claim (D-C) is classified, not refused.
5. **Tests (host).** Item 4's function over every state, driven through the `f_services` fixture (accept, `prepare_suspend` to Idle or Switch, the idle poll to Resuming, `finish_idle_resume` with the operation present): after the stop, no operation owner, a clear control, the queue entry removed and the ledger complete, one quantum ticket. Items 1–2 as a pure function over the queue and the outbox: a hold that pushes and ends is drained or staged, never left in the shared queue. The live interleavings (the guard-free CR3 window, IPI timing) are covered only by `--smp` boots; the assertion in item 1 makes a violation fail at the hold that caused it rather than at a later stop's commit.

**Review of §10.15 (2026-11-14).** Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5 at low effort, against `d92ead3`; checked by the coordinator. It confirms the direction and finds the rule broken today in two places the design did not name.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| C1 | High | A resumed wait returns to user mode without a drain: the stationary `resume_suspended` absorbs the wait's releases into the shared queue and the facade drops the guard; `authorize_return` does not drain. Against `ReturnReady`'s contract and rule 4; the most frequent such hold, at shutdown especially | Revision 2, item 1 names it |
| C2 | Medium | The remote-termination initiator's holds (the `CompleteRemoteStop` dispatch hold, the exception path's `Retry`, `Immediate` and `Remote`) end with the target's releases in the shared queue while T is released to await the stops; that they finalize after the acknowledgements is an accident of the first stop staging the whole queue | Revision 2, item 2 |
| D1 | Medium | Item 1's `drain_finalizers` would add family-G steps (teardown, release, finalize) to every safe-point hold, including the stop's prepare hold with a root-switch flight open | Revision 2, item 1: only the cleanup queue |
| D2 | Medium | Draining the initiator's holds would finalize the dead Process's handles before the stops are acknowledged, reversing today's order | Revision 2, item 2: they stage privately |
| D3 | Low | Staging should take only the stopped CPU's own pushes, and a CPU's outbox at its own precommit should be named | Revision 2, item 3 |
| — | — | The F-services cleanup queue needs the same rule, or a precommit's `absorb` can take another CPU's releases | Revision 2, item 1 |
| — | — | Enforcement: the runtime guard's `Drop` cannot drain (no carrier access); 80 lock sites, many ending early | Revision 2, item 1 |
| — | — | Item 4's state list lacks: an idle poll that returned `Switch` (unreachable), Running with a parked call, a terminal-owned continuation; the transfer must test the stopped Thread's own operation owner, not the global `service_state_is_quiescent` | Revision 2, item 4 |
| Q2 | Low | Overlapping stops can delay staged cleanup's finalization without bound (pre-existing) | Accepted (Low): finalization only, each stop bounded |
| Q3 | — | `settle_ended_parked_calls` may settle the record of an exited Thread still mid-yield on another CPU (pre-existing); `resume_parked_call` on a missing record unchecked | To be checked in this step before building |

**Revision 2 (supersedes items 1–4 above).**
1. **Every hold ends drained of the cleanup queue, not the steps.** One hold-end function on the carrier runs `drain_cleanup_queue` only (its cascade and deferred-interrupt retry; no teardown, release or finalize step) and then asserts the shared cleanup queue and the F-services queue empty. The resume hold (C1), the `SuspendCurrent`, `YieldCurrent` and `ServiceRendezvous` dispatch holds and every other hold that pushes end through it. The runtime guard's `Drop` asserts both queues empty as a backstop (a trait bound on the guarded state), so a path that skips the function fails at the hold that caused it.
2. **Holds that must not finalize yet stage privately.** `TerminateCurrent` holds, the remote-termination initiator's prepare hold and the exception path's `Retry`, `Immediate` and `Remote` holds move their pushes into a per-CPU outbox. The terminal handoff's first hold, and the termination's completion hold, drain it, preserving finalize-after-acknowledgement on purpose.
3. **The stop stages what is its own.** With items 1–2 the shared queue is empty at every hold end, so `stage_rendezvous_cleanup` takes only the stopped CPU's pushes. A non-empty outbox on the stopped CPU at its precommit is classified and panics with its fields (no reachable case found: a Stop never targets an exited Thread).
4. **The handoff, classified once,** as before, with the states added (idle poll returned `Switch`, Running with a parked call, terminal-owned continuation: the first two classified, the last a panic naming fields) and the transfer decided by the stopped Thread's own operation owner.
5. **Tests (host),** as before, plus: a resume hold whose wait completion pushes leaves both queues empty; initiator holds' releases finalize only after the acknowledgements; a precommit stages only its own CPU's pushes; the guard's backstop fires on an undrained hold; the transfer predicate is per Thread.

**As built (2026-11-14, M5 step 7).** Deepwyrm `f04ea04`, `bc9c5de`, `0757d11` and `b9095c5` (head `b9095c5da6f45cd3e303bedca159a9d9173f8561`), written by a worker (Claude Opus 5.5) in lane `lane/m5s7-holds` from `b3a78bf`, reviewed by the coordinator and integrated by fast-forward. `e7df793` and `4959a54` are a Q3 fix that `2f64b36` undoes; together they change no code, and their messages describe a defect that does not exist.

*Q3, checked first: neither half is a defect.* A drain can settle the parked record of an exited Thread still mid-yield on another CPU, but the record holds only a create's backing allocation and its quota reservation, and that CPU's next safe point sees the Stop before `resume_parked_call` takes a record. A missing record there panics (it is checked) and cannot be reached: both publishers (the termination dispatch and `terminate_exception_with_remote_stops`) publish the Stop inside the preparation's hold, so every later hold sees it.

*The cause of §10.13's third failure, found by item 4.* When the idle poll resumes the same Thread, `schedule_from_idle_on` clears the scheduler's suspended claim and leaves a Running one at once (`task/scheduler.rs`, `ResumeCurrent`), while the wait control stays `Resuming` until the resume's handoff hold. A Stop taken at the resume's safe point reached the precommit on its Running branch, which retired no control, and the commit's quiescence check failed with "remote stop released execution ownership before deferred cleanup quiesced". The Running branch now retires a `Resuming` control. From the code and a host test that reaches the state through the real scheduler; not reproduced live. The design review's explanation (the shared queue, about 55%) was not it, though its C1 and C2 were real.

1. *Hold end.* `end_hold(HoldEnd)` on the carrier: it absorbs the F-service queue, then `Drain` pops the cleanup queue only, `Stage` moves it into this CPU's outbox, and `Return` moves the outbox back and runs the full `drain_finalizers`, as a syscall return did; then both queues are asserted empty. The decisions and queue moves are in `syscall/hold_end.rs`, which the host builds. The runtime guard's `Drop` checks both queues through a `HoldEndCheck` bound on the guarded value and panics naming where the guard was taken. Routed with `Drain`: `ready_to_return` for `SuspendCurrent`, `YieldCurrent` and `ServiceRendezvous`; the resume hold (C1); each of the terminal reaper's holds (`0757d11`, found by the backstop on the first `--smp` boots). With `Return`: every `ReturnToCaller`, and the terminal handoff's first drain.
2. *Outbox.* `CleanupOutboxes`, one store tagged by CPU, sized by the registry. Staged: `TerminateCurrent` results, the initiator's prepare hold and the ProcessExit Retry hold, and the exception path's Retry, Immediate, Remote and completion holds. An initiator whose own completion returns `TerminateCurrent` stages again, and the terminal handoff's first hold drains it.
3. *The stop stages its own.* `stage_rendezvous_cleanup` is unchanged: the shared queue at a precommit now holds only that hold's pushes. A staged outbox at a precommit panics with its fields; the audit found a reachable case (D1 below).
4. *Classification.* `syscall/stop_handoff.rs`, `classify_stop_handoff`: Running, Running with a parked call, resumed with its operation unsettled, Pending with or without a replacement, Idle, Resuming. It panics naming the facts for a terminal-owned continuation, a staged outbox, an ambiguous owner, both claims being the Stop's, a Running claim with a Pending or Idle control, and a prepared switch (Clear with a suspended claim). The control is retired whenever it is not Clear, and the Thread's own operation is moved out whenever it has one, on either branch (D-B).
5. *Tests (host).* `hold_end`: the control table; a pushing hold ends drained or staged; initiator releases finalize only after the acknowledgements; a terminating initiator keeps them for the terminal handoff; outboxes keep other CPUs' releases in order. The guard's backstop fires on an undrained hold and names it. `stop_handoff`: every state with its actions, the per-Thread transfer predicate, the panics. `a_remote_stop_settles_every_handoff_state` drives a wait through the `f_services` fixture to each state and applies the stop. No host harness reaches the carrier's own routing, the trampoline's order or the guard-free CR3 window: boots only.

*Deviations from revision 2.* `end_hold` absorbs the F-service queue instead of only asserting it empty. A returning hold keeps the full drain with its steps. The reaper's later holds pop the cleanup queue while a root-switch flight may be open (the audit found the queue is empty there). Resuming is met on the Running branch. **Clear with a suspended claim is refused, not classified** (R2 below), against revision 2 item 4 and the step-6 review's D-C.

**Audit of §10.15 as built (2026-11-14).** Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5, which reported low effort, at `0757d11cb29804be2427734fcd9bd9b28853cdfa`; checked by the coordinator. It enumerated every acquisition of T (all in `primordial.rs`) and found none that can push a release and end outside a hold end, and it confirmed the ordering: outboxes are released only on their own CPU, and no hold between an initiator's Stage and its completion returns to a caller.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | High (panic; needs two Threads of one Process on different CPUs, which jobs cannot have today) | An initiator can be stopped between its prepare hold and its completion: `settle_dispatched_control` (`arch/x86_64/syscall/live.rs`) polls for a Stop before `complete_remote_stop`, and `terminal_stop_plan` does not ask whether the target CPU owns a pending termination. The precommit then panics on the staged outbox. Older than this step: before it the termination was never completed and its target's releases were swept before the acknowledgements | Scheduled: §10.16. The coordinator's first fix (complete, then take the Stop) was checked by the worker and rejected: a completion whose initiator's own Process was exited meanwhile trips "Process terminal control and deferred current ownership diverged" (`syscall/adapters.rs`) |
| R2 | High if reached (unsound) | Revision 2 classified Clear with a suspended claim as a prepared switch and let the stop proceed. It is reachable: after a preemption, yield or wait switch the destination's first safe point checks for a Stop before `complete_physical_switch_handoff`, with the outgoing Thread's suspended claim and `last_scheduled` still in place. The reaper would then resume the destination from the continuation it saved when it last switched out, which its run since has overwritten | Refused again by a named panic (`b9095c5`), as before this step. Scheduled: §10.16 |
| R3 | Medium | The exception path's Retry loop spins without polling the mailbox; a Stop published meanwhile is never acknowledged, and its initiator waits for it | Scheduled: §10.16. Not built, not tested |
| R1 | Low | The classification was passed `running = !was_suspended`, so three of its arms could not fire | Fixed: `b9095c5`, the precommit passes the Running claim compared with the Stop's on its own |
| R4 | Low | Three comments described a protocol the code does not have | Fixed: `b9095c5` |
| — | — | A completion returning `TerminateCurrent` while another initiator's Stop for the same Thread is pending would leave that Stop unacknowledged; not traced | Scheduled: §10.16 item 5 |

**Checked (development tier).** By the coordinator at `b9095c5`: kernel host tests (1,354), `xtask clippy`, `kernel-check` and `fmt`, all passing. At `0757d11` the lint ratchet failed (a wildcard arm in a test helper), which the worker's first report had as clean; fixed in `b9095c5`. Boots with Wyrmroot `420b2fb`, QEMU TCG: the coordinator's at `0757d11`, 3 of 3 on one CPU and 9 of 10 `--smp`; the worker's at `0757d11`, 1 and 6, and at `b9095c5`, 1 and 5 (`.tmp/dev-boot/1794716978157368182`, `…7043475692055`, `…7101758909277`, `…7163087041953`, `…7225468264057`, `…7287338205438`). No kernel panic in any. The one failure (`.tmp/dev-boot/1794716254908812197`) was `wait job=10 status=foreign`, §10.13's teardown-rate failure, which §10.14 owns. The shutdown panic appeared about once in 30 boots, so 21 clean `--smp` boots do not prove it gone, and none of D1, R2 or R3 was forced. The first five `--smp` boots of `f04ea04` stopped at the backstop (`.tmp/dev-boot/1794713476956809828` and four more; named in `1794714761951081410`). Step times were not re-measured: an unrelated job loaded the host, and three one-CPU boots of the baseline `b3a78bf` showed longest steps of 41 to 88 ms, as the lane did.

### 10.16 A Stop is published only to a CPU that can take it: design (2026-11-14)

From the coordinator, after the audit above. Not built; to be reviewed independently and decided by Mike. It answers D1, R2, R3 and the untraced case.

**What the three share.** Each is a state in which the stopped CPU cannot safely take a Stop, and each was found by the stopped side meeting it. §10.13's three failures were the same class, and each fix taught the precommit one more state. The kernel already has the other answer for one such state: `terminal_stop_plan` refuses to publish (`Err`, which the initiator's dispatch returns as `Retry`) when the target CPU has a root-switch flight open, when its `last_scheduled` is not the claim's Thread, or when it has no active root. The initiator releases T, polls its own mailbox and tries again.

**Design.**
1. **Stoppable is decided at publication, by one function.** A pure function over the target CPU's facts, read under T in `terminal_stop_plan`, says whether a Stop may be published to it. It keeps the three refusals above and adds two:
   - *the CPU owns a pending remote termination* (D1): a per-CPU fact under T, set in the prepare hold that stages and publishes, cleared in its completion hold;
   - *the CPU has a prepared switch that may have executed* (R2): its suspended claim is the target's while its Running claim is another Thread's and its wait control is not `Pending`. `Pending` means the block committed and `prepare_suspend` has not run, so the outgoing Thread still executes and the replacement is unentered, which the stop handles today. The same test guards the plan's "physical and logical both terminal" branch, which assumes an unentered replacement.
2. **No transition into an unstoppable state crosses a published Stop.** Every hold that moves a CPU into one (the termination dispatch, a preemption's or yield's switch preparation, `prepare_suspend`) checks the mailbox under T first and services a Stop instead. The dispatch does; the review confirms the others or the build adds the check.
3. **The retry ends without the initiator.** A pending termination ends when its targets acknowledge, and wait edges point backwards in T order, so there is no cycle (the worker's check (a)). A prepared switch ends at the destination's handoff hold. A Retry hold pushes nothing, so an initiator stopped while it retries has an empty outbox.
4. **The exception path's Retry loop polls** (R3), as the syscall loop does. On a Stop the excepting Thread is being ended by another termination: it services the Stop, and its own termination is dropped.
5. **The stopped side keeps its panics.** A staged outbox and a prepared switch at a precommit become invariants that item 1 upholds, and their comments say so. With item 1, no Stop is published to a CPU between its prepare and completion holds, and a completion that returns `TerminateCurrent` retires the Thread's Running claim in the same hold that clears the pending fact, so no later Stop can name it: the untraced case is closed if the review confirms that.
6. **Tests (host).** The function over each fact. Through the fixture: a termination whose target CPU is in each unstoppable state returns `Retry` and publishes nothing, and succeeds once the state ends; a prepared switch reached through a preemption or a yield, if the fixture can, not only a wait. No host harness reaches the exception loop or the trampoline: boots only, said as such.

**Questions for review.** Item 2 for every transition, with file and line. Whether a target that preempts or yields often can starve an initiator's retries. Whether reading another CPU's wait control and claims at publication is coherent (what else moves them, and under which lock). Whether a `Pending` control with a replacement can outlast the physical switch. What the exception path must restore when it drops its own termination for a Stop. Whether refusing at publication leaves any state the stop still meets unclassified.

**Review of §10.16 (2026-11-14).** Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5, which reported low effort, at `33cc63bccde20b4735696453cdb8349b2b64c197`; DD2's code facts and Wyrmroot's handling of `WOULD_BLOCK` checked by the coordinator. It confirms refusal as the mechanism for D1 (completing first is ruled out by the recorded assertion; carrying the termination through the stop teaches the stop one more state) and finds that refusal alone does not close R2.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| DD1 | High | Item 2 is false for every transition but the termination dispatch. `prepare_preemption`, `prepare_suspend`, the idle poll's switch and the exception path's prepare hold do not check the mailbox under T; their callers check only before taking T. So a Stop published while the CPU was stoppable is crossed by the switch that follows, and the precommit meets the prepared switch: this is R2, and item 1 cannot prevent it | Revision 2, item 2: the check is added in each hold |
| DD2 | High; reachable by a single-Thread job | A Stop met at the exception path's first safe point: `terminate_exception_with_remote_stops` returns, and both callers go on to `terminate_current`, which panics ("terminal handoff retained a scheduler-current Thread") with the Stop unacknowledged. A job that takes an unhandled exception or a resource fault while init terminates it can reach it; no poll sits between an unserved page fault and the exception. Older than this step | Revision 2, item 4 |
| DD3 | Medium | Item 4's poll must be a check under T at the top of each iteration's hold, before the preparation, and the exception path must set and clear the pending fact. Without the first, the exception path publishes its own Stops over another initiator's (the untraced case); with the new refusal and without it, two CPUs deadlock until the await's 20,000,000 spins end in a halt | Revision 2, items 1 and 4 |
| DD4 | Medium; needs three CPUs | The predicate left out the mailbox. A stopped CPU moves on to its next Thread before the initiator reclaims its mailbox (`await_remote_stop_permits` reclaims in CPU order), so a second termination of that Thread passes the plan and panics on `Busy` ("remote-stop publication failed") | Revision 2, item 1 |
| DD5 | Low | Only ProcessExit and the exception path turn the plan's `Err` into a retry. ProcessTerminate, TaskGroupTerminate and ThreadTerminate return `DW_STATUS_WOULD_BLOCK` to userspace, and Wyrmroot's `terminate_task_group` does not retry it (coordinator's check). More refusals mean more of them | Revision 2, item 3: for Mike |
| — | — | The R2 test must read the Running claim unfiltered (the plan filters it to terminal Threads), and "suspended is the target's and the control is Clear" matches the precommit's panic exactly. The "both terminal" branch needs the same guard, or it retires the Running claim of a destination already executing | Revision 2, item 1 |
| — | — | Dropping an exception's own termination for a Stop changes what a fault means: under a ThreadTerminate's Stop, a fault that would have ended the whole Process ends only that Thread | Revision 2, item 4: recorded |
| — | Low | The ProcessExit and exception loops have no bound; starvation is probabilistic only (T is a ticket lock and each window ends at a hold of the target's own) | Accepted (Low) |
| — | — | Not settled: whether a plan can target a CPU whose continuation a terminal handoff owns (`deferred_current`) | Revision 2, item 1 refuses it |
| — | — | Suggested defence in depth: the precommit compares the bound kernel stack with the claim's stack instead of trusting `physically_executing`, which is stale in the R2 window | Not taken: items 1–2 make the state unreachable and the named panic stays |

**Revision 2 (supersedes items 1–5 above). Approved by Mike, 2026-11-15, with item 3 as recommended: the kernel retries.**
1. **Stoppable, decided at publication by one pure function** over the target CPU's facts, all read under T. It refuses a CPU that: has a root-switch flight open, a `last_scheduled` other than the claim's Thread, or no active root (as today); owns a pending remote termination, a fact kept in the guarded per-CPU slot (not the facade field of today), set in the prepare hold that publishes and cleared in its completion hold, on the syscall and the exception path alike; has the target's suspended claim with a Clear wait control (a prepared switch), reading the Running claim unfiltered, with the same guard on the "both terminal" branch; has a mailbox that is not Idle; or has a deferred current.
2. **Every hold that makes a CPU unstoppable checks the mailbox under T first.** `prepare_preemption` (preemption and yield) returns to its caller, which polls next. `prepare_suspend` leaves the control `Pending`, which the precommit handles, and diverts to the rendezvous reaper through a new plan variant. The idle poll returns a variant that makes its caller poll, not one that relies on the IPI's latch. The exception path's prepare hold checks on every iteration (item 4). The termination dispatch already does.
3. **Where the retry lives (decided: in the kernel).** A refusal is a short wait that ends at a hold of the target's own, which the kernel can see and userspace cannot. Recommended: every termination syscall retries in the kernel as ProcessExit does (release T, check its own mailbox, try again), so `WOULD_BLOCK` is no longer returned for a refused stop plan. The alternative keeps `WOULD_BLOCK` and makes Wyrmroot retry it, which init does not do today even for the root-switch refusal.
4. **The exception path takes a Stop wherever it finds one.** `terminate_exception_with_remote_stops` returns whether a Stop was found, at its first synchronization and in each iteration's hold before the preparation; on a Stop its callers go to the rendezvous reaper (`handoff_current_rendezvous_from_carrier`) instead of `terminate_current`, and the exception's own termination is dropped. A Retry has changed nothing by then (`prepare_remote_process_exception` returns before it reserves), so nothing is undone. Recorded consequence: a Thread stopped by a ThreadTerminate while it takes a fatal fault is ended alone, and its Process is not.
5. **The stopped side keeps its panics,** now invariants: a staged outbox (items 1 and 4), a prepared switch (item 2). The untraced case is closed on the syscall path by item 1 (a Stop published before the prepare hold is seen by its check; after it, the pending fact refuses; the completion retires the caller's Running claim in the hold that clears the fact) and on the exception path by item 4.
6. **Tests (host).** The function over each fact, the mailbox and the deferred current included. Through the fixture: a termination whose target is in each unstoppable state publishes nothing and succeeds once the state ends; a Stop published before a preemption, a suspend and an idle switch is found by that hold's check and the transition is not made. The exception path, the trampoline's order and the mailbox reclaim order have no host harness: boots only, said as such.

**As built (2026-11-15).** Deepwyrm `bea8a57` (revision 2) and `3299f77` (the review's three changes), by an Opus worker (Claude Opus 5.5) in lane `lane/m5s7-stoppable`; reviewed, checked and integrated by the coordinator. No ABI crate changed, so Wyrmroot and the SDK keep their pin.
1. *The decision* is `decide_stop_publication` (`kernel/src/syscall/stop_publication.rs`), pure over `StopTargetFacts`; `terminal_stop_plan` gathers the facts and calls it. Refusals: root-switch flight, `last_scheduled`, no or foreign active root (as before); a pending termination (`CpuSharedState.pending_remote_termination`, set in the hold that publishes and cleared in the completion hold, syscall and exception path); a deferred current; a mailbox that is not Idle (`RendezvousMailbox::stop_is_idle`); the terminal Thread's suspended claim with a Clear control (the Running claim read unfiltered; the "both terminal" branch takes the same guard).
2. *The holds.* `prepare_preemption` returns `Return` and all four callers poll next; `prepare_suspend` returns the new `NativeSuspendPlan::ServiceRendezvous` with the control still `Pending`; the idle poll returns `NativeIdleSuspendPoll::ServiceRendezvous` with the control still `Idle`; the exception loop looks at the top of every iteration. Each reads the mailbox with one atomic load (`stop_requested`), which consumes nothing.
3. *The retry.* The three terminate syscalls return `Retry` on a refused plan and the dispatch handles it as for ProcessExit. A refused plan is computed whole before anything is reserved, published or marked, so a retry starts from unchanged state; each try resolves the handle again and so returns what a first try would at that moment.
4. *The exception path* returns `ExceptionTermination::{Terminated, Stopped}`; on `Stopped` the terminal reaper calls `service_current_rendezvous` (both reapers pivot to the same per-CPU stack, as `enter_scheduled_fresh_thread` already does). `settle_dispatched_control` now completes a remote stop before it looks at `ServiceRendezvous`, so a completed termination whose return is refused and whose exception finds a Stop reaches the reaper.
5. *The stopped side's panics* are unchanged; their comments name what upholds them.

*The coordinator's review of `7bc0eea` (now `bea8a57`), three changes, built in `50523ea` (now `3299f77`):*
- *F1.* The Stop acknowledgement wait and the five retry pauses spin with IF clear and never acknowledged a TLB shootdown (§9). `await_live_remote_stop` takes a spin service and the retry pause is one function; both call `service_local_tlb_shootdown` on every iteration. No root-switch flight is open, the CR3 is the resident Process root and no lock the service takes is held at any of the six sites.
- *F2.* The holds' check called `take_notification`, which swallows a pending Wake. No lost wake was found (the IPI's latch drives the idle rescan and the poll follows in the same hold), but a check should change nothing: it is now the one load above.
- *F3, an addition to item 1 by the coordinator.* A Running target is refused while its CPU still holds any suspended claim (`StopRefusal::SwitchUnfinished`): a switch whose destination has not completed its handoff hold, where the stop's retirement of the Running claim would fail (`SwitchPending`). Before, only code order kept a Stop from being taken there (the handoff hold directly follows the synchronization at all seven call sites, checked). Every state with a Running claim beside another Thread's suspended claim is ended by a hold of that CPU's own.

*Tests (host).* The function over every fact; through the fixture, a Stop for the outgoing Thread and for the destination refused across a wait's prepared switch and a yield and published after `complete_switch_on`; the mailbox Idle only after its reclaim, and the check consuming no Wake. Each fails without its change except the positive cases. No host harness reaches the four hold checks, the exception loop and its routing, the pending fact's set and clear, the retry loop, the trampoline's order or F1: boots only, and no boot was shown to force D1, R2, R3, DD2, DD4 or F3.

*Checked (development tier), by the coordinator at `50523ea`:* kernel host tests (1,375), `xtask clippy` with the ratchet unchanged (236, 87), `kernel-check`, `fmt`. Re-signing changed the hashes and not the code (`git diff 50523ea 3299f77 -- . ':!Plans'` is empty).

*Boots, with Wyrmroot `b0d76c5`.* The worker's at `7bc0eea`: one on one CPU and ten `--smp`, all passing. At `50523ea`: one on one CPU passing; ten `--smp`, two failing with `unavailable: memory`. The host was by then at a load of 8 to 19 from unrelated jobs, so the coordinator booted `c45799e` and `50523ea` alternately, twelve `--smp` each: two baseline failures and one of the build, none with a kernel panic (§9).

*Diagnosis of the build's failure (`.tmp/dev-boot/1794748499790404399`; diagnostic lane `lane/m5s7-stop-diag2`, `46cfe39` to `95cf307` on `50523ea`, not integrated, 22 `--smp` boots).* The coordinator first read its paused state as a CPU waiting for a Stop that was not coming. That was wrong: `CR3=0x95000` is system-init's root, the two CPUs in the kernel hold consecutive tickets for T with nobody holding it, and init had answered the next request before the pause. Nothing was stuck. With every termination syscall timed, the longest in a passing boot was about 6 ms and none was refused or retried; in a failing boot (`1794753210841040967`) the terminate took about 6 ms and the missing second lay outside it. The same three failure forms occur at baseline and on kernels of 2026-11-14. The diagnostic boots also found the deadline-wake panic (§9).

*Security review of §10.16 as built.* By the coordinator (Claude Mythos 5.2), 2026-11-15, at `7bc0eeacb003d83656ba3fa4a1ddc396d05c064f` and `50523ea209ad3585f7b7476807126857385d98a7` (now `bea8a57b764a6d70dc4a25e534eac85854d6704d` and `3299f774b0ed862c8c9a0bec73f48920bfa43b2b`), the whole diff from `c45799e` read against revision 2; the author was the worker (Mike, 2026-11-15, §9). No Critical or High finding left open.

### 10.17 Step 7: security review of §10.14 and §10.15 as built (2026-11-15)

Independent, read-only, Claude Opus 5.5, 2026-11-15, at Deepwyrm `f4a2cb28cce114d73948707cc7d3b064b274dd5f` (the diffs `b3a78bf..b9095c5` and `33cc63b..c1537d7`). **Effort:** the coordinator requested high effort in the spawn; the reviewer reported its configured effort as low all the same, so the request did not take, and §9's row on review effort stays. Findings checked against the code by the coordinator. §10.16 is not built and is not covered.

| # | Sev. | Finding | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|
| B-1 | Medium (High if an unprivileged Process can hold a running Process's AddressRegion with map rights; not established) | A regression from §10.14: the first teardown step calls `begin_retiring` without the shootdown-initiator lock S, and `begin_retiring` refuses a root whose mutation is still `Prepared` or `Published`; the step panics on any refusal ("an owed root did not become retiring"). A Process mutating another's region publishes a shootdown and waits without T or P; if the target exits and its last resident leaves before the initiator completes, the dead root is queued with the mutation still in flight, and the next drain with a teardown allowance panics. Before §10.14 a step never touched the coherency phase, and only the retire did, under S (D1 option B4) | Fixed: Deepwyrm `ea1200d`. A step that meets a mutation in flight does nothing and stays queued, as for a resident (`begin_teardown_step`); every other refusal still panics and the retire keeps S. Two host tests, one of which fails without the decision. No boot reaches the interleaving |
| R-B1 | Low | A table step's error after earlier frees panics the caller; no job layout was found to reach one (no user large leaf is written, nothing clears an intermediate entry without reclaiming it) | Accepted (Low): a kernel inconsistency either way |
| R-B2 | Low | The retired-root proof on a retiring root with a non-zero user entry returns after its transaction began; the transaction's drop resets it | Accepted (Low): reachable only with wrong counts |
| R-A1 | Low | The reaper's later holds run `drain_cleanup_queue`, and so one `retry_deferred_interrupt`, with a root-switch flight open; the queue is empty there | Accepted (Low): older than this step |
| Q1 | — | Whether an unprivileged job can hold a running Process's AddressRegion with map rights (Wyrmroot's grants) | No, at Wyrmroot `b0d76c5`: the loader maps the image and then moves the root into the child's own init message or closes it before the first Thread starts, and a job's own root handle carries no TRANSFER right. So B-1 was Medium and latent, reachable only through a future grant |
| Q2 | — | Whether a family-G initiator's own Process can become quiescent while its shootdown is `Published` (a Stop taken inside the await), which would reach B-1 within one Process | No: the wait services only its CPU's TLB mailbox with interrupts off and takes no Stop, and the waiting CPU is resident on its own root throughout. Nothing stays `Published`: the initiator completes once every target has acknowledged, holding only S, and panics after 1,000,000 wait steps |

*B-1 as fixed, checked (development tier).* By the coordinator at `ea1200d`: kernel host tests (1,366), `xtask clippy`, `kernel-check` and `fmt`. The worker's boots with Wyrmroot `b0d76c5`: one on one CPU and six `--smp`, all passing, no panic, teardown owed 0.06 to 0.20 s (`.tmp/dev-boot/1794724292956084146` to `…4683049888324`). A retiring root refuses map, unmap and protect with `BAD_STATE` on every path, before anything changes.

No finding on its other items: residency is entered before every CR3 load and cleared only after the switch away, the stop path's guard-free window included; `begin_retiring` is one way and closes `enter`, `prepare_mutation` and `root_for_process`, through which every cross-Process session, usercopy, pin and fault passes; nothing reads a dead root's leaves after phase 1; `RoleSlot.tables`' two meanings have no reader that confuses them, and a reused slot's generation cannot match a stale parent; the table step's bounds and termination hold; the detached removal runs `unmap`'s transaction unchanged and the normal `unmap` path is unchanged; the outboxes cannot overflow the registry's capacity and are released only on their own CPU; teardown steps run only in full drains, in the places they ran before. It did not re-enumerate every acquisition of T (it relied on §10.15's audit), and it did not read `rendezvous.rs`, the precommit and commit bodies beyond the classification, or the host tests. It found nothing that changes D1, R2, R3, DD2 or DD4, and §10.16 would not close B-1.

### 10.19 Step 8: a floor under the rate of owed finalization: design (2026-11-15)

From the coordinator, on Mike's decision (§10.18). Not built.

**What was measured.** In `.tmp/dev-boot/1794755755750099404` finalization stayed owed for 2.9 s. The boot's finalize work was ordinary (1,660 steps, 0.54 s in all, longest step 0.7 ms; a boot with 0.16 s owed took 1,562 steps and 0.49 s). So the steps were not slow: they were far apart. A step is taken once per drain (§10.12 item 5), and drains come from syscall returns and refused faults on any CPU, from the idle BSP, and from the BSP's timer gate. With the BSP in user code that makes no syscalls (the check's CPU hogs) and nothing else entering the kernel, the only drain is the BSP's timer gate, once per interrupt: the default quantum is 5 ms, so about 200 steps a second, and one fully committed 256 MiB object is about 130 steps. An AP's timer gate grants nothing (`allow_teardown_step_here`), and an idle AP takes no step (`idle_work_remains` is BSP-only). This is step-5 review R5; that this boot is that case fits the figures and is not shown directly.

**Design.**
1. **More than one step at the BSP's timer gate.** When the BSP's timer gate drains and finalization is still owed after that drain's own step, it takes further finalize steps, up to `FINALIZE_TICK_STEPS` (4) in all for that gate. Every other drain keeps its one step.
2. **A hold stays one step long.** Each further step runs in its own hold of T and P, released between steps, so the longest hold is still one step (§10.12's K R-1: no finalize step exceeded 0.9 ms) and waiters on the ticket lock get their turn in between. Each further hold looks for a Stop first and stops stepping if one is requested (§10.16 item 2's read), and ends drained under §10.15's rule.
3. **The floor.** While finalization is owed, the BSP is in user code and it takes a timer interrupt at least once per quantum, at least 4 steps (2,048 entries, about 8 MiB of committed pages) are taken per 5 ms: a job's largest budget, 512 MiB (131,072 pages, about 257 steps), is finalized within 65 quanta, 0.33 s, plus its wait behind objects queued before it (the queue is FIFO and the jobs' limit bounds the total). The cost is taken from whatever runs on the BSP: at the measured 0.32 ms average step, about 1.3 ms in each 5 ms while finalization is owed, and at the worst measured step (0.9 ms) 3.6 ms, with interrupts off across the gate as today.
4. **Not changed.** Teardown's allowance and its one step per BSP tick (§10.10, §10.14); the AP gates; the idle loops; the step's budget (`FINALIZE_STEP_ENTRIES`); what a syscall or fault refuses while finalization is owed.
5. **Report.** `[steps]` already reports `call-steps finalize` and `owed-longest-us finalize`; nothing is added unless the worker finds the gate's steps cannot otherwise be told apart from the others in a boot.
6. **Tests (host).** The count of steps a drain takes, as a pure function of the drain's kind and of whether finalization is still owed, over every case; through the memory authority, four steps free four budgets and leave the indexes whole, and a queue that empties early stops the loop. The gate's loop itself is target-only: boots, said as such.

**To be established by the worker before it builds (stop if one fails).** Which function is the BSP timer gate's drain, and that an AP's gate never reaches it. That a timer interrupt reaches the BSP at least once per quantum while it is in user code and finalization is owed, including when its Thread is the only runnable one; if none is guaranteed, say what arms one, and stop: the floor then needs a deadline of its own, which is a design change. That releasing T between steps inside the gate is sound there (what the gate holds, what a Stop or a reschedule published between two steps meets). That four steps do not overrun what the gate's stack and the step report's nesting assume (§10.12 review D4).

**Expected, to be measured, not promised:** with the hogs on the BSP the longest owed stretch falls by about four; §10.18's 39 ordinary boots (0.14 to 0.97 s) should stay under 1 s and the 2.9 s case should not recur in 40 `--smp` boots.

**Check of the design against the code (2026-11-15): the premise is false; the design above is withdrawn.** By the worker (Claude Opus 5.5) at `a78c5bf`, before building; nothing was built. One pass of the BSP's user-mode timer gate (`native_runtime_timer_pre_iret`) already runs `drain_finalizers` up to four times in three holds of T: through the q35 retirement safe point inside `with_synchronized_runtime_at_safe_point` (reached from `authorize_timer_return`, `has_reschedule_request` and `prepare_quantum`) and through `service_pending_timer_expiries_on_bootstrap`. Each drain ends with a finalize step.
